| Lead review requests that fail are reset at most twice per head and then alert once, every per-PR skip of the enqueue writes one `[skip <class>]` line, and a stale `running` row is reclaimed (BH-5779) | [../notes/2026-10-09-BH-5779-lead-review-stuck.md](../notes/2026-10-09-BH-5779-lead-review-stuck.md) | `bin/lead-review-enqueue.sh`: a head whose request answers `existing` with status `failed` is reset through `POST /api/lead-reviews/<id>/reset`, at most `LEAD_REVIEW_FAILED_RETRY_MAX` times (lane knob, default 2, 0 = never reset), counted in `state/lead-review-enqueue/failed-retry.tsv`; the next failure sends one alert `lead-failed-<slug>-<n>-<sha12>` that names the reason; a reset counts toward `LEAD_REVIEW_ENQUEUE_MAX_PER_TICK` and the 24 h tripwire; an environment failure (`checkout`, `no-checkout`, `empty`, `review-failed: env:`) is left to `bin/lead-review-land.sh` (BH-5793) with one `[skip env-failed]` line. Every `continue` of the per-PR loop writes one line `<repo>#<n> @<h12>: <reason> [skip <class>]`, classes `draft`, `malformed`, `unreadable`, `no-approve`, `verdict`, `replayed`, `ci`, `risk-signed`, `head-moved`, `inbuild-wait`, `existing`, `deferred`, `tripped`, `self`, `failed-exhausted`, `reset-refused`, `env-failed`; a lead-authored PR the builder sent back names the author and the `bin/lead-verdict.sh request` command. Each tick posts `POST /api/lead-reviews/reclaim-stale` (fail-open) and the summary adds `failed_reset=`, `failed_alerted=`, `reclaimed=`. `worker/jobs/src/routes/lead-reviews.js`: `POST /api/lead-reviews/reclaim-stale` (lead token, else 403) sends a `running` row with no `heartbeat_at` (else `updated_at`) newer than settings `lead_review_running_deadline_s` (fallback 3600, floored at `lead_review_claim_stale_s`) back to `requested`, and a second stale run of the same row to `failed`, detail `stale: …`. Verified by `cd worker/jobs && node --test test/lead-reviews-reclaim.bh-5779.test.js`, `HARNESS_TEST_SANDBOX=1 bash tests/lead-review-enqueue-failed-retry.bh-5779-test.sh` and `HARNESS_TEST_SANDBOX=1 bash tests/lead-review-enqueue-no-silent-skip.bh-5779-test.sh` |

Nothing merges unread (the operator, #5803): any unread file that is not generated or a fixture, test or not, is split required with the files listed (`ldp_build ... any`, and the lane refuses a rc 0 with `LDP_UNREAD` set). Selector: the section titled `BOX SWITCHES`; columns BOX (links `/machines`), SWITCH (links `/config/setting/<key>`, the row history), VALUE, SET BY (the row's `actor`), SET AT (`updated_at`). Listed: key `switch.<name>.<box>` with value 1/on/true (trimmed, any case). Not listed: the fleet row `switch.<name>`, an OFF row, a box switch that is only a `flags/` file on that box. The page filter narrows both tables (BOX SWITCHES matches name, box and set by). States: skeleton while the first read runs; `could not load` with RETRY on a failed read (never `no box switch is on`); `no box switch is on` for zero ON rows. Box side of item 2: `HARNESS=<root> bin/alerts-groom.sh`, then `logs/alerts-groom.log` has `flag groom: removed N alerted-* flag(s) dated before <day>`. | Ported-flag routes answer a D1 overload with a 503 (BH-6805): `GET /api/flags/<name>`, `PUT /api/flags` and `DELETE /api/flags` for a ported name (`paused-*`, `automerge-disabled[-*]`, `lanedown-*`, `dispatch-mode-*`) answer 503 `{"error":"harness_flags unavailable","detail":"…overloaded…","retryable":true,"d1_attempts":3}` when D1 is still overloaded after the BH-6690 retries, never a 404 or 409; when the PUT or DELETE batch has committed and only the read-back overloads, the answer is 200 `{"flag":null,"written":true}` with no "overloaded" and no `retryable`, so `bin/lib/jobs/req.sh` does not re-send the write (`written:true`) (no screen; preconditions: harness-jobs worker, bearer `HARNESS_SECRET`) | [../notes/2026-10-15-BH-6805-flags-overload-503.md](../notes/2026-10-15-BH-6805-flags-overload-503.md) | API: `GET /api/flags/paused-<p>` with Authorization `Bearer <secret>`. Normal answers: 200 `{flag,as_of}`, or 404 for no row. During an overload: 503 with the body above, and one worker log line `d1-overload: harness_flags GET paused-<p> answered 503 after 3 attempt(s)`. Box side: `bin/lib/jobs/req.sh` retries a 503 whose body holds "overloaded" once, then `flag_fetch` answers from `state/flags-cache/`. A 200 `written:true` is a landed write: `flag_set`, `flag_clear` and the import cache it with no row and return 0 (`_flag_written`). What usually lies: a 404 means "no row", not "D1 failed"; a non-overload error (a SQL error) still throws and reaches Sentry. Live check after deploy: Sentry CROMFORD-HARNESS-Z has 0 new `readPortedFlag` events in 24 h. Verified by `worker/jobs/test/flags-overload.bh-6805.test.js` and `tests/flags-written.bh-6805-test.sh` |

Precondition: `staff_ticks` rows exist only after the hub ran `bin/staff.sh tick` with this code (one row per tick past the lock), so a fresh deploy shows "no tick recorded yet" until the next */15 staff cron tick; cost appears only for ticks that made a model call and whose cost rows were pushed. Composer: `textarea[aria-label="message to <agent>"]` and a SEND button. Note copy: `waiting for the next tick`, `agent stopped: kept, read when it resumes`, `in tick <key> now`, `answered in tick <key>` (the key links `/staff/tick/<id>` once the tick row exists), `no answer in tick <key>`, `not answered after <n> tries`. A banner `agent stopped (<flag>): a message is kept and read when it resumes` shows when the GET answers `stopped`. A note state `read in tick <key>` shows for a read note whose reply row is not on the page. Empty state `no messages yet`; an `OLDER` button pages back by `next`. Archie has no CHAT section and no tab. Reads `GET /api/coord/staff/chat?agent=<agent>[&before=]`, writes `POST /api/coord/staff/chat` (worker `worker/jobs/src/routes/staff-chat.js`, table `staff_chat`). What usually lies: a file-only kill flag with no `pause_flags` row is not seen by the send, so the note shows as waiting; the reply arrives up to 15 min later on the `*/15` staff cron cadence. Reads `GET /api/coord/staff/autonomy?agent=<name>` (worker `worker/jobs/src/routes/staff-autonomy.js`; 404 for an unknown agent and for archie, 503 naming `staff_autonomy_changes` when the schema is not applied). Writes `POST /api/coord/staff/agents/autonomy` `{agent, autonomy, reason}` and `POST /api/coord/staff/classes/delegation` `{agent, class, decision, reason}`; the proxy refuses a blank reason (400) and stamps the actor. Selectors: buttons `CHANGE LEVEL…`, `DELEGATE…`, `ASK FIRST…`; modal `.modal[role="dialog"]`, `select[aria-label="level"]`, `input[aria-label="reason"]`, confirm `SAVE`. What usually lies: a class whose NAME matches the protected pattern (`cost`, `deploy`, `merge`, `spend`, `prod`, `client`, `release`, …) shows never_delegable even with `never_delegable = 0`, so a QA row for quartermaster must not be called `cost-report` (use e.g. `rotate-key`); every seeded agent is capped at augmentation, so CHANGE LEVEL… offers one level. ## Driving conventions

- **Drive the real script, never a paraphrase of it.** The suites under `tests/`
  extract the production block VERBATIM by its `# >>> name` / `# <<< name`
  markers, or run the whole script against a scratch `$HARNESS`. A test that
  restates the logic passes while the lane is broken.
- **Assert on artifacts, never on source text.** What a check is worth is the
  contents of `logs/ALERTS.log`, `logs/pipeline-health.log` and the files under
  `state/` — grepping the script for a string proves only that somebody typed it.
- **Pin the clock.** Every fixture age comes from one `NOW=$(date +%s)` read
  (`bin/test-determinism-lint.sh` enforces this). A suite that reads the wall
  clock twice picks its own red day.
- **Fail-open is the property under test.** For anything that can SUPPRESS an
  alert, the negatives carry the weight: an API outage, a missing row, an
  unparseable record must all still page. Test those before the happy path.
- **Non-destructive only.** These lanes write to the coordination DB and the
  console. Fixtures run against a `mktemp -d` tree with stubbed `curl`/`ssh`/`gh`;
  nothing in `tests/` may touch a real box or a real ledger.
- **run-build's marker blocks live in `bin/lib/rb/`.** Since the BH-572 cut
  `bin/run-build.sh` runs its stages as bare `rb_<name>` calls, so its marker
  blocks live in `bin/lib/rb/` and suites read them through `tests/lib/rb-all.sh`
  (`$RB_ALL`, the pipeline as one text), never by grepping the driver.
- **Run the suite the safe way.** [tests/README.md](../../tests/README.md) has the runners, the root guard, and how to read the two safety lanes above.

## Generated files

Machine-written; regenerate, never hand-edit. `bin/batch-resolve.sh` and
`mc_regen_table` read this table **from this file** — since BH-1175 the index
below is not committed, so a parser pointed only at the rendered README would
find nothing and the batch merge train would switch itself off. Nothing may
follow this table in this file but more rows.

The index is git-ignored: a regenerate refreshes the working tree and stages
nothing, which is the point. What a build commits is its own
`docs/verification-map/entries/<TICKET-KEY>.md`.

**And since BH-1247 so is every other row below.** The ten crontabs,
`catalog/lanes.json` and `env-templates/global.env.example` were committed
renders until that ticket, which made each of them a shared mutable cell every
build wrote: a PR that edited a lane and did not re-render turned red, and once
it merged every other open PR inherited the red on a file its ticket never
mentioned (three merge trains, 2026-09-21). They are git-ignored now. The rows
stay here because this table is also where an operator reads how to rebuild one
— and because the table is what switches this repo's batch merge train on
(BH-865) — but nothing in it can conflict any more, so the train never has to
resolve one. Adding a lane is a file under `catalog/lanes.d/` and a file under
`cron/lanes/`, and nothing else.

**Every path here is literal — no globs.** `bin/batch-resolve.sh`'s
`regen_cmd_for` and `path_allowed` match with shell `case`, where `*` crosses
`/`, so a `cron/*.cron` row would also claim `cron/lanes/<lane>.cron`. A
conflicted path with a regenerate command is resolved by taking one side
(`git checkout --theirs`), which for a hand-written FRAGMENT would drop one
approved PR's cron change with the render and every check still green. The ten
crontabs are therefore written out one by one; they all rebuild with the same
command.

**But do not run that command inside a box checkout** (BH-1247). It writes the
ten git-ignored `cron/<box>.cron` into the tree, where `git status` will not
show them and `git pull` will never remove them. `bin/render-crontab.sh` refuses
to use such a file while `cron/lanes/` is present, so a box still installs the
right crontab — but the files have no reason to exist there. To SEE a render,
use `bin/render-cron.sh <box>`, `bin/render-cron.sh --out-dir <dir>` or
`bin/render-diff.sh`.

**Three rows here are still COMMITTED. The first is `rules/CLAUDE.md`** (BH-1336). It is rendered
from `rules/topics/` by `bin/render-rules.sh`, and five scripts copy it verbatim
onto every box and every builder — `bin/update-runners.sh`,
`bin/sync-agent-rules.sh`, `fly/builder-runner.sh`, `bin/rules-drift-check.sh`
and `bin/context-signature.sh` — so it cannot leave git until they render it
themselves (BH-1336 workstream B). It is in this table anyway, because a
committed render is exactly the file a train or a rebase would otherwise HAND
MERGE: with the row, a conflict on it is resolved by taking one side and running
`bin/render-rules.sh --write` over the merged topics, which is drift-free and
keeps both siblings' edits. It needs no merge attribute for the same reason —
union-concatenating a document with a generated table of contents produces a
file nobody wrote. `docs/PR-OWNERSHIP.md` (BH-5235.2) is the second committed
render, for the same reason: `bin/pr-owner-sweep.sh`, `bin/lib/pr-health.sh`
and the docs link it by path and readers browse it on GitHub, so it stays in git, and a conflict on it is resolved
by `bin/render-doc-sections.sh --write` over the merged `docs/PR-OWNERSHIP.d/`.

**`worker/jobs/schema.sql` is NOT in git** (BH-6005). It is composed from
`worker/jobs/schema.d/NNNN-<name>.sql` by `bin/schema-build.sh` and is
git-ignored: committed, it was the factory's most frequent merge conflict. Each
reader composes it where it reads it: `bin/harness-sync.sh`'s deploy (`--to`
into its snapshot, before the D1 apply and `wrangler deploy`), CI and the lints
(`--ref` for a base revision), and the test corpus (`tests/lib/jobs-schema.sh`,
`worker/jobs/test/lib/jobs-schema.js`). `bin/schema-build.sh --check` refuses it
back in git. Its row below is unmarked, so a build's diff never carries it.

The third column says so. A row marked `committed` is a render this repo's CI
checks, so a build commits it; an unmarked row is reset out of a build's diff
by the `# >>> generated no-commit` block of `bin/run-build.sh` (unless the diff
is nothing but declared files), because the conductor regenerates it on merge
(BH-1680). The merge readers ignore the
